\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left(\Delta\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right)^{2}\\
\leq\lambda\left(\|\theta^{\star}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}-\|\theta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\right)+{\frac{2}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\Delta^{\top}}.

(E.1)

Recall that
\check{\Theta}=M(\Theta)=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\Theta^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}(\Theta^{\prime}\mid\Theta)
. Then using the fact that
\nabla_{\check{\Theta}}\mathcal{L}(\check{\Theta}\mid\Theta)=0
, we have

\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\check{\theta}{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]=0.

(E.2)

Therefore, we can rearrange

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}

\displaystyle={\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}}-{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}}

\displaystyle\quad+{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}}-{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\check{\theta}{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\right],

where the last term is zero following (
E.2
). Define the following quantity

\displaystyle K^{w}_{\Psi}

\displaystyle=K^{w}_{\Psi}(\Theta)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\Psi(t_{n})\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right].

(E.3)

Then, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\star}\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}=\theta^{\star}\Delta_{\Psi}+\Delta_{w}+\Delta_{\varepsilon}+(\check{\theta}-{\theta}^{\star})K^{w}_{\Psi},

(E.4)

where
\Delta_{\Psi}
is defined in (
4.5
),
\Delta_{w}
is defined in (
4.6
), and
\Delta_{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
4.4
).
Plug the result (
E.4
) back to (
E.1
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left(\Delta\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left(\|\theta^{\star}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}-\|\theta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\right)+2\sigma_{\max}(K^{w}_{\Psi})\|\Delta\|_{2}\|\check{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\|\Delta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\left(\|\Delta_{\varepsilon}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}+\|\Delta_{w}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}+\|\theta^{\star}\Delta_{\Psi}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\right),

(E.5)

where

\|\Delta_{\varepsilon}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}=\max_{j=1,\ldots,p}\|\Delta_{\varepsilon,j}\|_{\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}}^{*}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}}^{*}}
is the dual norm of
\|\cdot\|_{\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}}}
.

Define
S
to be the support set of
\theta^{\star}
and
S^{c}
be the complement of
S
.
Using the fact that

λ
≥4(∥Δ_
ε
∥_∞,
^

K

_Ψ^*
+
∥Δ_w∥_∞,
^

K

_Ψ^*
+∥
θ
^⋆Δ_Ψ∥_∞,
^

K

_Ψ^*),
we have

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\|\theta^{\star}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}-\|\theta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\right)+2\|\Delta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}

\displaystyle\left(\|\Delta_{\varepsilon}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}+\|\Delta_{w}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}+\|\theta^{\star}\Delta_{\Psi}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left(\|\theta^{\star}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}-\|\theta^{\star}+\Delta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\right)+\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\Delta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}

\displaystyle=\lambda\left(\|\theta^{\star}_{S}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}-\|(\theta^{\star}+\Delta)_{S}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}-\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda}{2}\left(\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}+\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}-\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}.

(E.6)

From the result of (
E.6
) and the facts that (i) the left hand side of (
E.6
) is lower bounded by
0
and (ii)

\lambda\geq\frac{4}{\sqrt{s}}\frac{\sigma_{\max}{(K_{\Psi})}}{\max_{j}\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\|\check{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}},

we can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}+2\sigma_{\max}(K^{w}_{\Psi})\|\Delta\|_{2}{\|\check{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}}.

Define
K_{\Psi}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\Psi(t_{n})\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}]
and it follows that
\sigma_{\max}(K^{w}_{\Psi})\leq\sigma_{\max}(K_{\Psi})
. Therefore, we can upper bound the above display as

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}+2\sigma_{\max}(K_{\Psi})\|\Delta\|_{2}{\|\check{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\lambda}{2}\max_{j}\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})\sqrt{s}\|\Delta\|_{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\max_{j}\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})\sqrt{s}\|\Delta\|_{2}

\displaystyle=2\lambda\max_{j}\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})\sqrt{s}\|\Delta\|_{2}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}+\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}=\|\Delta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\leq 5\max_{j}\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})\sqrt{s}\|\Delta\|_{2}.

(E.7)

Hence, combining (
E.6
), we can write (
E.5
) as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left(\Delta\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda\|\Delta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}+{2\sigma_{\max}(K_{\Psi}^{w})\|\check{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}}\|\Delta\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\Delta\|_{2}\big\{5\lambda\max_{j}\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\psi_{j}})\sqrt{s}+\sigma_{\max}(K_{\Psi})\|\check{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}\big\}

(E.8)

Under Assumption
6
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{\Delta\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\alpha\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}-\tau\|\Delta\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\alpha\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}-25\max_{j}\sigma_{\max}^{2}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})s\tau\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}.

Combining the above result with (
E.8
), we can conclude that

\|\Delta\|_{2}\leq\frac{4}{\alpha}\left(5\lambda\max_{j}\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\psi_{j}})\sqrt{s}+\sigma_{\max}(K_{\Psi})\|\check{\theta}-\theta^{\star}\|_{2}\right).

E.2
One-step update of
\sigma^{2}

Define

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{n,\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(Y_{n,i}-Y_{n-1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta^{\ell}_{ij}\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})\right)^{2},\quad R_{n,\ell}=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(Y_{n,i}-Y_{n-1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta^{\ell}_{ij}{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})\right)^{2}.

(E.9)

Lemma E.1
.

Suppose that Assumption
8
,
9
hold. Assume that there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that for
n=1,\ldots,N
,
\widehat{R}_{n,\ell},R_{n,\ell}\leq c_{0}^{2}p
. Define
\delta_{1}=\max_{i}|\!|\!|X_{i}-\widehat{X}_{i}|\!|\!|_{{2}}
,

\delta_{2}=\left|\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\left\{\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\right]\right\}\right|.

Suppose that for each
\ell
, the difference
\sum_{i,j}\|\theta_{ij}^{\ell}-\theta^{\ell\star}_{ij}\|_{2}^{2}\leq r_{0}^{2}
. Suppose that
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\max_{ij}|\dot{g}_{j}(X_{i}(t))|\leq D
,
\pi_{\min}=\min_{Q\in\Omega}\min_{\ell}\pi_{\ell}
, and

\delta_{\min}=\min_{\Theta\in\Omega}\min_{n=1,\ldots,N}P(Z_{n},Y_{n};\Theta)>0
. Given
\Theta
, define
\widehat{\Theta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\widetilde{\Theta}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}_{N}(\widetilde{\Theta}\mid\Theta)
and
\check{\Theta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\widetilde{\Theta}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\Theta}\mid\Theta)
.
Then,

\left|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|\leq\frac{C\sqrt{ms}k\delta_{1}}{N}\max_{j}\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}+\delta_{2}+C^{\prime}k\zeta^{-8}\pi_{\min}^{-2}\left\{1-(\zeta\pi_{\min})^{2}\right\}^{r-1}+\left|\check{\sigma}^{2}-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|,

where
C
is a constant depending on
(\Theta^{\star},r_{0},c_{0},D)
and
C^{\prime}
is a constant depending on
(c_{0},\delta_{\min})
.

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.

Fixing parameters
Q
,
\theta^{\ell}
for
\ell=1,\ldots,k
, the optimal parameter
\sigma^{2}
can be represented as

\widehat{\sigma}^{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{{\sigma^{\prime}}^{2}}-\frac{pN}{2}\log 2{\sigma^{\prime}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4{\sigma^{\prime}}^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(Y_{n,i}-Y_{n-1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta^{\ell}_{ij}\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})\right)^{2}.

This implies that

\widehat{\sigma}^{2}=\frac{1}{2Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(Y_{n,i}-Y_{n-1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta^{\ell}_{ij}\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})\right)^{2}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}-{\sigma}^{\star 2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})\left(\widehat{R}_{n,\ell}-R_{n,\ell}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\right]\right)-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|

\displaystyle=T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}.

Subsequently, we bound each term separately. First, we write

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left(\widehat{R}_{n,\ell}-R_{n,\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left(\widehat{R}_{n,\ell}^{1/2}+{R}_{n,\ell}^{1/2}\right)\bigg\|\sum_{i}\theta_{i}^{\ell}\left(\widehat{\Psi}_{i}(t_{n})-\Psi_{i}(t_{n})\right)\bigg\|_{2},

where
\theta_{i}^{\ell}=(\theta_{1i}^{\ell\top},\ldots,\theta_{pi}^{\ell\top})
. Using the fact that
\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}\leq 1
and
R^{1/2}_{n,\ell},\widehat{R}_{n,\ell}^{1/2}\leq c_{0}p
, the above display can be bounded as

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bigg\|\sum_{i}\theta_{i}^{\ell}\left(\widehat{\Psi}_{i}(t_{n})-\Psi_{i}(t_{n})\right)\bigg\|_{2}.

(E.10)

We can apply Hölder’s inequality and obtain the following upper bound

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{i}\theta_{i}^{\ell}\left(\widehat{\Psi}_{i}(t_{n})-\Psi_{i}(t_{n})\right)\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}}\left\{\sum_{i}\theta_{i^{\prime}i}^{\ell}\left(\widehat{\Psi}_{i}(t_{n})-\Psi_{i}(t_{n})\right)\right\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}}\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}\|_{1}^{2}}\max_{i,j}\left|\Psi_{ij}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}_{ij}(t_{n})\right|,

(E.11)

where
\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}=(\theta_{i^{\prime}1}^{\ell\top},\ldots,\theta_{i^{\prime}p}^{\ell\top})
.
Note that

\displaystyle\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell\star}-\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}\|_{1}\leq\max_{j}\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell\star}-\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}\leq 5\max_{j}\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\sqrt{ms}\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell\star}-\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}\|_{2},

(E.12)

where the last inequality follows from (
E.7
). Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{1}+5\max_{j}\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\sqrt{ms}\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell\star}-\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}\|_{2},

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i^{\prime}}\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i^{\prime}}\left\{\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{1}^{2}+25ms\max_{j}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\right)^{2}\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell\star}-\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i^{\prime}}\|\theta_{i^{\prime}\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{1}^{2}+50ms\max_{j}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\right)^{2}r_{0}^{2}.

Apply (
E.23
) to (
E.11
) and plug the results back to (
E.10
), we have

T_{1}\leq\frac{C\sqrt{ms}k\delta_{1}}{N}\max_{j}\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})},

where
C=C(r_{0},c_{0},\Theta^{\star},D)
,
m
is the number of basis functions,
k
is the number of states and
\delta_{1}=\max_{i}|\!|\!|X_{i}-\widehat{X}_{i}|\!|\!|_{{2}}
.

Note that by assumption, we have
T_{2}\leq\delta_{2}
. Apply Lemma
4.4
, we can bound
T_{3}
as

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})-w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\right\}R_{n}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}k\zeta^{-8}\pi_{\min}^{-2}\left\{1-(\zeta\pi_{\min})^{2}\right\}^{r-1},

where
C^{\prime}=C^{\prime}(c_{0},\delta_{\min})
.
Recall that
\check{\Theta}=\arg\max_{\Theta^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}(\Theta^{\prime}\mid\Theta)
and we have
\check{\sigma}^{2}

\check{\sigma}^{2}=\frac{1}{Np}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}[w_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}].

Therefore,
T_{4}=\left|\check{\sigma}^{2}-\sigma^{2}\right|
.
Combining the results from
T_{1}
to
T_{4}
, we have

\left|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|\leq\frac{C\sqrt{ms}k\delta_{1}}{N}\max_{j}\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}+\delta_{2}+C^{\prime}k\zeta^{-8}\pi_{\min}^{-2}\left\{1-(\zeta\pi_{\min})^{2}\right\}^{r-1}+\left|\check{\sigma}^{2}-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|.

∎

E.3
One-step update of
Q

This section shows the one-step update of the transition rate matrix
Q
. We begin with stating the main result.

Lemma E.2
.

Suppose that
Z(t)
is strictly stationary and irreducible and
Z(0)
is sampled from the stationary distribution. Given
\Theta
, let
\check{\Theta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\Theta^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}(\Theta\mid\Theta^{\star})
and ,
\widehat{\Theta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\widetilde{\Theta}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}_{N}(\widetilde{\Theta}\mid\Theta)
, and

\delta_{3}=\max_{i,j}\frac{1}{N}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]\right|.

Then, there exists a constant
C=C(Q,Y_{0}^{N})<\infty
such that for
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
we have

\left|\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|\leq C\delta_{3}+\left|\check{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|.

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.

From
Bladt and Sørensen (2005)
;
Liu et al. (2015)
, the expected number of transition from
\ell
state to
\ell^{\prime}
state given the observations can be written as:

\mathbb{E}[{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(1)\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i,j}{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})\mathbb{E}\left[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(t_{n}-t_{n-1})\mid Z(t_{n-1})=i,Z(t_{n})=j;Q\right],

where
m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(t)
is the number of transition from state
\ell
to
\ell^{\prime}
in time
[0,t]
.
We define the similar version for the truncated version:

\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(1)\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i,j}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})\mathbb{E}\left[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(t_{n}-t_{n-1})\mid Z(t_{n-1})=i,Z(t_{n})=j;Q\right].

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}(1)\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i,j}{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})\mathbb{E}\left[\tau_{\ell}(t_{n}-t_{n-1})\mid Z(t_{n-1})=i,Z(t_{n})=j;Q\right];

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}(1)\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{i,j}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})\mathbb{E}\left[\tau_{\ell}(t_{n}-t_{n-1})\mid Z(t_{n-1})=i,Z(t_{n})=j;Q\right].

Fixing parameters of
\theta_{ij}^{\ell}
and
\sigma^{2}
, the optimal
\widehat{Q}
that optimize
\mathcal{L}_{N}(\Theta^{\prime}\mid\Theta)
is

\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(1)\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]}{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}(1)\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]},

for
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
and
\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell}=-\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}
.
For notation simplicity, starting from below, we express
\tau_{\ell}(1)
as
{\tau}_{\ell}
,
\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}(1)
as
\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}
,
\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(1)
as
\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}
,
m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(1)
as
m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}
.
Hence, for each
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]}{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]}-\frac{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]]}{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{n};\Theta]]}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\frac{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]]}{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{n};\Theta]]}-\frac{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]]}{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{n};\Theta]]}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\frac{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]]}{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{n};\Theta]]}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|

\displaystyle=T_{5}+T_{6}+T_{7}.

First, we can decompose
T_{5}
into two terms:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]}{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]}-\frac{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]]}{\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{n};\Theta]]}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell};\Theta]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]]\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]}{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell};\Theta]\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell};\Theta]\right|.

Due to the time-homogeneity of Markov chain and
t_{n}-t_{n-1}=h
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(h)\mid Z(h)=i,Z(0)=j;Q\right]\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n}).

By Fubini’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]\right]=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(h)\mid Z(h)=i,Z(0)=j;Q\right]\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})].

Combining above two terms, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]\right|.

(E.13)

Similarly, apply the time-homogeneity property of Markov-chain again, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[\tau_{\ell}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]\right|.

(E.14)

Combining the results of (
E.13
)– (
E.14
), we can upper bound
T_{5}
as

\displaystyle T_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i,j}\frac{\mathbb{E}[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q]+\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q]}{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell};\Theta]}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]\right|,

(E.15)

where
\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]/\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]
. Furthermore, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]

\displaystyle=\int P\left(Z(t_{n})=j,Z(t_{n-1})=i\mid Y_{(n-r)\vee 0}^{(n+r)\wedge N};\Theta\right)P\left(Y_{(n-r)\vee 0}^{(n+r)\wedge N};\Theta\right)\mathrm{d}Y_{(n-r)\vee 0}\cdots\mathrm{d}Y_{(n+r)\wedge N}

\displaystyle=P(Z(t_{n})=j,Z(t_{n-1})=i;Q)

\displaystyle=P(Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q),

for
n=1,\ldots,N
. The last equality follows because
Z(t)
for
t\geq 0
is stationary.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell};\Theta]

\displaystyle=N\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q]P(Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[{\tau}_{\ell};\Theta]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}\mid Z(0)=i;\Theta]\pi_{i}

\displaystyle>\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}\mid Z(0)=\ell;\Theta]\pi_{\ell}

Note that
\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}\mid Z(0)=\ell;\Theta]
is greater than minimum of the expected holding time of
Z(t)
at state
\ell
and
1
. Since the holding time of
Z(t)
at state follows a exponential distribution with rate
-q_{\ell\ell}=\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}
, we know that the expected holding time is
-1/q_{\ell\ell}
. Hence the above term is further lower bounded as

\displaystyle>\left(\frac{1}{\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}}\wedge 1\right)\pi_{\min}>0.

(E.16)

Therefore, we can ensure that

\sum_{i,j}\frac{\mathbb{E}[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q]+\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q]}{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell};\Theta]},

is finite and well behaved. Compute the exact upper bound with respect to
Q,Y_{0}^{N}
would throw us into technical weeds, we hence assume there is a constant
C=C(Q,Y_{0}^{N})
such that

\sum_{i,j}\frac{\mathbb{E}[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q]+\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}(h)\mid Z(h)=j,Z(0)=i;Q]}{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell};\Theta]}\leq C.

As a result, we can write (
E.15
) as

\displaystyle T_{5}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{\max_{i,j}\frac{1}{N}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]\right|\right\}\leq C\delta_{3}.

(E.17)

Next, we want to show that
T_{6}=0
. This is because

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]

\displaystyle=\int P\left(Z(t_{n})=j,Z(t_{n=1})=i\mid Y_{(n-r)\vee 0}^{(n+r)\wedge N};\Theta\right)P(Y_{(n-r)\vee 0}^{(n+r)\wedge N})\mathrm{d}Y_{(n-r)\vee 0}\cdots\mathrm{d}Y_{(n+r)\wedge N}

\displaystyle=P\left(Z(t_{n})=j,Z(t_{n-1})=i;\Theta\right)=\mathbb{E}[{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})],

for
n=1,\ldots,N
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(h)\mid Z(h)=i,Z(0)=j;Q\right]\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}(h)\mid Z(h)=i,Z(0)=j;Q\right]\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[{m}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]\right].

Similarly, we can show that

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[{\tau}_{\ell}\mid Y_{0}^{N};\Theta]\right].

Hence, we have
T_{6}=0
.

Finally, recall that
\check{\Theta}=\arg\max_{\Theta^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}(\Theta^{\prime}\mid\Theta)
and it follows that

\check{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}[m_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}\mid\Theta]}{\mathbb{E}[\tau_{\ell}\mid\Theta]}.

Hence
T_{7}=\left|\check{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|
. Combining
T_{5}
to
T_{7}
together, we arrive at

\left|\widehat{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|\leq C\delta_{3}+\left|\check{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|.

∎

E.4
Auxiliary Lemmas for the analysis of one-step update

Lemma E.3
.

Given a fixed
i=1,\ldots,p
, define
Y_{n}^{\Delta}=Y_{n,i}-Y_{n-1,i}
for
n=2,\ldots,N
. Let
\max_{\ell=1,\ldots,k}\max_{n=1,\ldots,N}\left|Y_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta_{i}^{\ell}\Psi(t_{n})\right|=c_{0}
and
|g_{j}(X_{i}(t))|\leq B
for
i=1,\ldots,p
and
j=1,\ldots,m
. Recall that
\Theta=\{\theta^{\ell}_{ij};i,j=1,\ldots,p,\ell=1,\ldots,k\}\cup\{Q\}
and suppose that
Q
satisfies Assumption
9
. Define the coefficient

C=\left(\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\right)Bc_{0}\frac{10}{\delta_{\min}}\zeta^{-8}\pi_{\min}^{-2},

where
\zeta
is the mixing coefficient defined in (
4.7
),
\delta_{\min}
is defined in Lemma
4.4
and
\pi_{\min}
is defined in (
4.8
).
Then, we have

\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})-w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\right\}\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\ell\star}_{i}\Psi(t_{n})\right\}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\bigg\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\leq C\sqrt{m}\left\{1-(\zeta\pi_{\min}^{2})\right\}^{r-1}.

Proof of Lemma
E.3
.

Write

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{w}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})-w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\right\}\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\ell\star}_{i}\Psi(t_{n})\right\}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\bigg\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})-w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\right|\left|{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\ell\star}_{i}\Psi(t_{n})\right|\|\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}

(E.18)

Note that

\displaystyle\|\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\leq\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\|\Psi_{j}(t_{n})\|_{2}\leq\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}B\sqrt{m}.

(E.19)

Apply (
E.19
) to (
E.18
), we can obtain

\|\Delta_{w}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\leq\left(\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\right)\frac{Bc_{0}\sqrt{m}}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})-w_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\right|

Apply Lemma
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{w}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\leq\left(\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\right)Bc_{0}\sqrt{m}\frac{10}{\delta_{\min}}\zeta^{-8}\pi_{\min}^{-2}\left\{1-(\zeta\pi_{\min}^{2})\right\}^{r-1}.

∎

Lemma E.4
.

Observe two stochastic processes
X_{t}
and
\widehat{X}_{t}
on
[0,1]
. Suppose that
\max_{j=1,\ldots,p}|\!|\!|X_{i}-\widehat{X}_{i}|\!|\!|_{{2}}=\delta
, and
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|g_{j}(X_{i}(t))|\leq B
,
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|g^{\prime}_{j}(X_{i}(t))|\leq D
for
i=1,\ldots,p
and
k=1,\ldots,m
. Assume that
\theta^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times pm}
is
ms
-sparse with
s\leq p
.
Then,

\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n}){\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\}\bigg\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\leq\max_{j}\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}BD\delta m\sqrt{s}.

Proof of Lemma
E.4
.

Write

\displaystyle\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})

\displaystyle\{{\Psi}(t_{n}){\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\}\bigg\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\max_{j}\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n}){\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\}\bigg\|_{\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}}^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n}){\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\underbrace{\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\}{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\bigg\|_{2}}_{T_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\underbrace{\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\}\bigg\|_{2}}_{T_{2}}.

(E.20)

Note that by triangle inequality, we can write

\displaystyle T_{1}=\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\}{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\}\right|\|{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\}\right|B\sqrt{m}.

(E.21)

Define the support of
\theta^{\star}
as
{\mathcal{S}}
. Then, (
E.21
) is equivalent as

\displaystyle\left|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\}\right|B\sqrt{m}

\displaystyle=\left|\theta_{\mathcal{S}}^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})[{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})]_{{\mathcal{S}}}\right|B\sqrt{m}

Apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can further bound the above term as

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{\mathcal{S}}^{\star}\|_{2}\bigg\|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})[{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})]_{{\mathcal{S}}}\bigg\|_{2}B\sqrt{m}

\displaystyle\leq Bm\sqrt{s}\|\theta_{\mathcal{S}}^{\star}\|_{2}\bigg\|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})[{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})]_{{\mathcal{S}}}\bigg\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq Bm\sqrt{s}\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}\bigg\|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left|{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right|\bigg\|_{\infty}.

(E.22)

Note that for each
i=1,\ldots,p
and
j=1,\ldots,m
, we can write

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left|{\Psi}_{ij}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}_{ij}(t_{n})\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left|g_{ij}(X_{i,u})-g_{ij}(\widehat{X}_{i,u})\right|\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\left|g_{ij}(X_{j,u})-g_{ij}(\widehat{X}_{i,u})\right|\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left|D(X_{i,u}-\widehat{X}_{i,u})\right|\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}\mathrm{d}u\right)^{1/2}\left\{\int_{0}^{T}(X_{i,u}-\widehat{X}_{i,u})^{2}\mathrm{d}u\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=D|\!|\!|X_{i}-\widehat{X}_{i}|\!|\!|_{{2}}\leq D\delta

(E.23)

Plug the result of (
E.23
) into (
E.22
) and then into (
E.21
), we obtain

T_{1}=\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n})-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\}{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\bigg\|_{2}\leq BD\delta m\sqrt{s}\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}.

(E.24)

Similarly, we can write

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{n}\left|\theta^{\star}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right|\bigg\|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left|{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\right|\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{n}\left|\theta^{\star}_{\mathcal{S}}(t_{n})[\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})]_{{\mathcal{S}}}\right|\bigg\|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left|{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\right|\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}B\sqrt{s}m\bigg\|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left|{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\right|\bigg\|_{\infty}.

Plug result of (
E.23
) into the above term, we arrive at

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}B\sqrt{s}mD\delta

(E.25)

Once we obtain the upper bound of
T_{1}
in (
E.24
), and
T_{2}
in (
E.25
), we can bound the right hand side of (
E.20
) and arrive at

\bigg\|\theta^{\star}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\{{\Psi}(t_{n}){\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})^{\top}\}\bigg\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\leq\max_{j}\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}BD\delta m\sqrt{s}.

∎

Lemma E.5
.

Suppose that
N>4
, then for some universal constants
C_{1},C_{2}
, we have

P\left(\max_{i,j}\frac{1}{N}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})-{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})\right|>C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}}\right)\leq k^{2}\exp(-C_{2}).

Proof of Lemma
E.5
.

The proof of Lemma
F.1
explains that
\widehat{w}_{\ell,\Theta}(t_{n})
for
n=1,\ldots,N
are stationary mixing process with sub-Weibull(
2
) norm bounded by
1
and the decay coefficient
\gamma_{1}=1
. Therefore,
1/\gamma=1/\gamma_{1}+1/\gamma_{2}=2/3
. Apply Lemma
I.2
, we have

P\left(\frac{1}{N}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})-{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})\right|>C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}}\right)\leq\exp(-C_{2}).

Taking the maximum over
i,j=1,\ldots,k
and apply union bound on the right side of the above equation, we have

P\left(\max_{i,j}\frac{1}{N}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})-{w}_{\Theta,ij}(t_{n})\right|>C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}}\right)\leq k^{2}\exp(-C_{2}).

∎

Lemma E.6
.

Let
\kappa=\max_{j}\left\{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})/\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})\right\}
and assume
\sum_{i,j}\|\theta_{ij}^{\ell}-\theta_{ij}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}^{2}\leq r_{0}^{2}
,
N>4
. Then, we have

P\left(\frac{1}{Np}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\right]\right|>\frac{C_{1}(2\sigma^{2}+\rho^{2}+ms\kappa r_{0}^{2}B^{2})}{\sqrt{N}}\right)\leq\exp(-C_{2}),

for some absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.6
.

Write
\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}

\displaystyle\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}

\displaystyle=\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(Y_{n,i}-Y_{n-1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta_{ij}^{\ell}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(X_{i}(t_{n})+\varepsilon_{n,i}-X_{i}(t_{n-1})-\varepsilon_{n-1,i}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta_{ij}^{\ell}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})\right)^{2}.

Recall the definition of
\rho_{n,i}
in (
F.1
), the above term is equal to

\displaystyle=\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left\{\varepsilon_{n,i}+\varepsilon_{n-1,i}+\rho_{n,i}+\sum_{j}(\theta_{ij}^{\ell}-\theta_{ij}^{\ell\star})\Psi_{j}(t_{n})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\varepsilon_{n,i}^{2}+\varepsilon_{n-1,i}^{2}+\rho_{n,i}^{2}+\|\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{1}^{2}\|\Psi(t_{n})\|_{\infty}^{2}\right),

where
\|\Psi(t_{n})\|_{\infty}\leq B
.
From (
E.12
), we can further bound

\|\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{1}\leq 5\max_{j}\frac{\sigma_{\max}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{j}})}\sqrt{ms}\|\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\leq 5\kappa\sqrt{ms}r_{0}.

Hence, apply Lemma
I.3
twice, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\|_{\psi_{2/3}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}\|_{\psi_{2}}\sum_{i}\left(\|\varepsilon_{n,i}\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}+\|\varepsilon_{n-1,i}\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}+\|\rho_{i,n}\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}+\|\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{1}^{2}\|\|\Psi(t_{n})\|_{\infty}\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Cp\left(2\sigma^{2}+\rho^{2}+ms\kappa^{2}r_{0}^{2}B^{2}\right).

Apply Lemma
I.4
and triangular inequality, we have

\frac{1}{p}\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}-\mathbb{E}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\|_{\psi_{2/3}}\leq 2C\left(2\sigma^{2}+\rho^{2}+ms\kappa^{2}r_{0}^{2}B^{2}\right).

Note that the sequence
\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
is
\beta
-mixing and stationary following the argument in Lemma
F.1
. Therefore, we can apply Lemma
I.2
with
1/\gamma=1/\gamma_{1}+1/\gamma_{2}=1+3/2=5/2
,
N>4
and obtain

P\left(\frac{1}{Np}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})R_{n,\ell}\right]\right|>\frac{C_{1}(2\sigma^{2}+\rho^{2}+ms\kappa r_{0}^{2}B^{2})}{\sqrt{N}}\right)\leq\exp(-C_{2}).

∎

Appendix F
Proof of deviation bound

The goal is to show that the Assumption
7
hold with the desired
\lambda,\alpha,\tau
by applying Lemma
I.2
. We begin with stating the main result followed by the analysis.

Lemma F.1
.

Suppose that
(X(t))_{t\geq 0}
is a stationary and
\beta
-mixing process with rate
\beta(\ell)\leq c_{1}\exp(-c\ell)
for some absolute constants
c,c_{1}>0
.
Let
N\gtrsim m^{4}(\log p)^{4}
. Given a fixed
i
, assume that
Z(t_{n})=\ell
and
define

\rho_{n}=\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\theta_{i}^{Z(u)\star}g(X_{u})\mathrm{d}u-\theta_{i}^{\ell\star}\Psi(t_{n})

for
n=1,\ldots,N
.
Suppose that
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|g_{j}(X_{i}(t))|\leq B
for
i=1,\ldots,p
and
j=1,\ldots,m

\max_{n}|\rho_{n}|\leq\rho
. Let
r
be a positive integer,
c_{2},c_{3}
be absolute constants. and
{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}=Y_{n,i}-{Y}_{n-1,i}
.
Define

\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})=P\left(Z(t_{n})=\ell\mid Y_{(n+r)\wedge N},Y_{\left\{(n+r)\wedge N\right\}-1},\ldots,Y_{n},\ldots,Y_{\left\{(n-r)\vee 0\right\}+1},Y_{(n-r)\vee 0}\right).

Then, we can define

\Delta_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\ell\star}_{i}\Psi(t_{n})\right\}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{{Y}_{n}^{\Delta}-\theta^{\ell\star}_{i}\Psi(t_{n})\right\}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right].

Then,

\|\Delta_{\varepsilon}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\geq\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}\frac{c_{2}\sqrt{m}B(2\sigma^{\star}+\rho)}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{i}})}\sqrt{\frac{m\log p}{N}},

with probability smaller than
6\exp(-c_{3}m\log p)
.

F.1
Proof of Lemma
F.1

Recall that

\displaystyle Y_{n,i}-Y_{n-1,i}-\theta^{\ell\star}_{i}\Psi(t_{n})=\varepsilon_{n,i}+\varepsilon_{n-1,i}+\underbrace{\sum_{j}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\theta_{ij}^{Z(u)\star}g(X_{j}(u))\mathrm{d}u-\theta^{\ell\star}\Psi(t_{n})}_{:=\rho_{n,i}}.

(F.1)

In the following, since we only consider single index
i
, we drop the index
i
in
\rho_{n,i}
as
\rho_{n}
for simplicity.

First, we decompose

\Delta_{\varepsilon}=\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}+\Delta_{\varepsilon_{2}}+\Delta_{\rho},

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right];

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varepsilon_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n-1,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n-1,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right];

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\rho_{n}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\rho_{n}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right].

The last term is the error due to approximation.
Since

\|\Delta_{\varepsilon}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\leq\|\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}+\|\Delta_{\varepsilon_{2}}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}+\|\Delta_{\rho}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}},

(F.2)

we can bound
\|\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}
,
\|\Delta_{\varepsilon_{2}}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}
, and
\|\Delta_{\rho}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}
separately by applying Lemma
F.2
. To apply the lemma, we verify the following conditions.

Step 1: Control the tail behavior of
\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}
.
Let
\gamma=2/3
, for each
n=1,\ldots,N
and
j=1,\ldots,p
, we can write

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]\right\|_{\psi_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\|_{\psi_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]\|_{\psi_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\|_{\psi_{\gamma}},

(F.3)

where the last inequality follows by Lemma
I.4
. Apply Lemma
I.3
, we have

\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\|_{\psi_{\gamma}}\leq 2^{3/2}\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\|_{\psi_{2}}\|\varepsilon_{n,i}\|_{\psi_{2}}\|\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\|_{\psi_{2}}.

(F.4)

Since
\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\in[0,1]
, we have
\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq 1
.
\varepsilon_{n,i}
is a centered Gaussian random variable with variance
(\sigma^{\star})^{2}
and hence it follows that
\|\varepsilon_{n,i}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\sigma^{\star}
for some constant
C>0
. Finally,

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{j}(t_{n})\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}\|\nu^{\top}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}\sup_{p\geq 1}p^{-1/2}\left\{\mathbb{E}|\nu^{\top}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})|^{p}\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}\sup_{p\geq 1}p^{-1/2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\|\nu\|_{1}^{p}\|\Psi_{j}(t_{n})\|_{\infty}^{p}\right\}^{1/p}\leq B\sqrt{m}.

(F.5)

Collecting the above results (
F.3
)–(
F.5
), we arrive at

\left\|\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]\right\|_{\psi_{\gamma}}\leq C_{1}\sigma^{\star}\sqrt{m}B,

for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
.
Hence we can conclude that for each
n=1,\ldots,N
and
j=1,\ldots,p
,
\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi_{j}(t_{n})^{\top}\right]
is a sub-Weibull(
2/3
) random variable with sub-Weibull norm bounded by
C_{1}\sigma^{\star}\sqrt{m}B
.

Step 2: Statistical properties of
\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}
.
In the following step, we verify the mixing and stationary conditions.

We first show that the product process
\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right]\}_{n=1,\ldots,N}
is strictly stationary. Recall that for each
i=1,\ldots,p
, we have
\Psi_{i}(t_{n})=\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}g(X_{i}(u))\mathrm{d}u
. Apply Lemma
I.7
, we have
\Psi(t_{n})
is a strictly stationary process. Since two stochastic processes
\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n=1,\ldots,N}
and
\{X(t_{n})\}_{n=1,\ldots,N}
are independent, and hence
\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n=1,\ldots,N}
and
\{\Psi(t_{n})\}_{n=1,\ldots,N}
are independent. Therefore, the joint process

\left\{\left(X({t_{n^{\prime}}}),\varepsilon_{n^{\prime}},\Psi(t_{n});n^{\prime}=(n-r)\vee 1,\ldots,(n+r)\wedge N\right)\right\}_{n=1,\ldots,N},

is strictly stationary process.

Recall that
Y_{n}=X(t_{n})+\varepsilon_{n}
and
note that the process
\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})
is a measurable mapping of
(Y_{(n-r)\vee 1},\ldots,Y_{(n+r)\wedge N})
, and therefore, the process
\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})\}_{n=1,\ldots,N}
is strictly stationary. By definition of the stationary process, the expectation
\mathbb{E}\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})\}
is a constant across
n=1,\ldots,N
. Hence, we can conclude that

\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right]\}_{n=1,\ldots,N}
is strictly stationary.

In the next step, we verify the mixing property. Define the filtration
\mathcal{F}^{\varepsilon}_{-\infty,n}=\sigma(\{\varepsilon_{j}:j\leq n\})
,
\mathcal{F}^{\varepsilon}_{n,\infty}=\sigma(\{\varepsilon_{j}:j\geq n\})
,
\mathcal{F}^{X}_{-\infty,t}=\sigma(\{X(u):u\leq t\})
, and
\mathcal{F}^{X}_{t,\infty}=\sigma(\{X(u):u\geq t\})
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,n}=\sigma(\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{j})\varepsilon_{j,i}\Psi(t_{j})^{\top}:j\leq n\})
and
\mathcal{F}_{n,\infty}=\sigma(\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{j})\varepsilon_{j,i}\Psi(t_{j})^{\top}:j\geq n\})
. It follows that for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,n}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,t_{n+r}}^{X}\vee\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,n+r}^{\varepsilon};

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{n+j,\infty}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{t_{n+j-r},\infty}^{X}\vee\mathcal{F}_{n+j-r,\infty}^{\varepsilon}.

Therefore, from Definition
6
, we have

\displaystyle\beta(j):=\sup_{n}\beta(\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,n},\mathcal{F}_{n+j,\infty})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n}\beta\left(\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,t_{n+r}}^{X}\vee\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,n+r}^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{F}_{t_{n+j-r},\infty}^{X}\vee\mathcal{F}_{n+j-r,\infty}^{\varepsilon}\right).

Apply Lemma
I.5
, above display can be further bounded as

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n}\beta\left(\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,t_{n+r}}^{X},\mathcal{F}_{t_{n+j-r},\infty}^{X}\right)+\sup_{n}\beta\left(\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,n}^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{F}_{n+j-r,\infty}^{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{n}\beta\left(\mathcal{F}_{-\infty,t_{n+r}}^{X},\mathcal{F}_{t_{n+j-r},\infty}^{X}\right)+0

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\exp(-C_{2}(j-2r))

\displaystyle=C_{3}\exp(C_{2}r)\exp(-C_{2}j).

If
r
is a constant, then
\exp(C_{2}r)
is well-behaved. Therefore, we can conclude that the sequence
\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})-\mathbb{E}\left\{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\varepsilon_{n,i}\Psi(t_{n})\right\}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
is geometrically
\beta
-mixing.

Step 3: Uniform concentration of
\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}
.
Using the results from
Step 1–2
, we can apply Lemma
F.2
: Compute
1/\gamma=1+3/2=5/2
and note that
N=C_{0}(m\log p)^{4}
, we have

\displaystyle P\left(\|\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\geq\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}\frac{C_{5}\sqrt{m}B\sigma^{\star}}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{{\Psi}_{i}})}\sqrt{\frac{m\log p}{N}}\right)\leq 2\exp(-C_{6}m\log p).

(F.6)

Step 4: Uniform concentration of
\Delta_{\varepsilon_{2}}
We can find the bound for
\|\Delta_{\varepsilon_{2}}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}
similarly and obtain

\displaystyle P\left(\|\Delta_{\varepsilon_{2}}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\geq\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}\frac{C_{5}\sqrt{m}B\sigma^{\star}}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{{\Psi}_{i}})}\sqrt{\frac{m\log p}{N}}\right)\leq 2\exp(-C_{6}m\log p).

(F.7)

Step 5: Uniform concentration of
\Delta_{\rho}
.
The steps to show uniform concentration property of
\Delta_{\rho}
follows exactly the same as
Step 1–3
, where we show the uniform concentration of
\Delta_{\varepsilon_{1}}
. It is easy to verify that for each
j=1,\ldots,p
and
n=1,\ldots,N
,
\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\rho_{n}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\rho_{n}\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right]
is sub-Weibull(
2/3
) with sub-Weibull norm bounded by
C_{4}\sqrt{m}B\rho
. Therefore, we can apply Lemma
F.2
and obtain

\displaystyle P\left(\|\Delta_{\rho}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\geq\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}\frac{C_{7}\sqrt{m}B\rho}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{{\Psi}_{i}})}\sqrt{\frac{m\log p}{N}}\right)\leq 2\exp(-C_{8}m\log p).

(F.8)

Step 6: Take the union bound.

Combining the results from (
F.2
), (
F.6
)– (
F.8
), we can conclude that

P\left(\|\Delta_{\varepsilon}\|_{\infty,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}^{*}}\geq\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}\frac{c_{2}\sqrt{m}B(2\sigma^{\star}+\rho)}{\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{i}})}\sqrt{\frac{m\log p}{N}}\right)\leq 6\exp(-c_{3}m\log p).

Lemma F.2
.

Let
A=\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{p}\}
be a set of
m\times m
positive definite matrices, with
\max_{i}\sigma_{\max}(A_{i})<c_{0}
. Define
\|W\|_{\infty,A}=\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}(W_{i}^{\top}A_{i}W_{i})^{1/2}
, with
W=(W_{1}^{\top},\ldots,W_{p}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{pm}
being a stacked vector and
W_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
for
i=1,\ldots,p
.
Let
Suppose that
(W_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{pm})_{n=1,\ldots,N}
is strictly stationary, geometrically
\beta
-mixing with rate
\gamma_{1}
and is zero-mean. For each
i=1,\ldots,p
,
W_{i}
is sub-Weibull(
\gamma_{2}
) with sub-Weibull norm bounded by
K_{1}
. Define
1/\gamma=1/\gamma_{1}+1/\gamma_{2}
. There exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
depending on
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
such that if
N=C_{1}(m\log p)^{2/\gamma-1}
then

P\left(\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}{\sum_{n=1}^{N}W_{n}}\bigg\|_{\infty,A}\geq{C_{2}K_{1}c_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{m\log p}{N}}\right)\leq 2\exp(-C_{3}m\log p).

Proof of Lemma
F.2
.

Define
S_{W}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}W_{n}
and
S_{W_{i}}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}W_{n,i}
, we can write

\|S_{W}\|_{\infty,A}\leq c_{0}\|S_{W}\|_{\infty,2}=c_{0}\max_{i}\|S_{W_{i}}\|_{2}

Let
\mathcal{N}(1/2)
be the
1/2
-net( See chapter 5 of
Wainwright (2019)
) of a unit ball on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. It follows that
for each
i
, we can write

\|S_{W_{i}}\|_{2}=\sup_{\|u\|_{2}\leq 1}u^{\top}S_{W_{i}}\leq\sup_{u\in\mathcal{N}(1/2)}\left|u^{\top}S_{W_{i}}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\|S_{W_{i}}\|_{2}.

(F.9)

Moving the last term in the right hand side to the left hand side, we yield ∥S_W_i∥_2≤2sup_u∈
N
(1/2) —u^⊤S_W_i—.
Hence, for any
t>0

P\left(\|S_{W_{i}}\|_{2}>2t\right)\leq P\left(\sup_{u\in\mathcal{N}(1/2)}\left|u^{\top}S_{W_{i}}\right|>t\right).

(F.10)

Since
\|u^{\top}S_{W_{i}}\|_{\psi_{\gamma_{2}}}\leq\|S_{W_{i}}\|_{\psi_{\gamma_{2}}}\leq K_{1}
for any
u\in\mathcal{N}(1/2)
, we can apply Lemma
I.2
and obtain that

P\left(\left|u^{\top}S_{W_{i}}\right|>t\right)\leq N\exp\left(-\frac{(tN)^{\gamma}}{K_{1}^{\gamma}C_{4}}\right)+\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}N}{K_{1}^{2}C_{2}}\right).

Since
\left|\mathcal{N}(1/2)\right|\leq 5^{m}
, taking the union bound over all
u\in\mathcal{N}(1/2)
, we have

P\left(\sup_{u\in\mathcal{N}(1/2)}\left|u^{\top}S_{W_{i}}\right|>t\right)\leq N5^{m}\exp\left(-\frac{(tN)^{\gamma}}{K_{1}^{\gamma}C_{4}}\right)+5^{m}\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}N}{K_{1}^{2}C_{2}}\right).

Then, combining the results of (
F.9
) and (
F.10
), we have

P\left(\|S_{W}\|_{\infty,A}>2c_{0}t\right)\leq P\left(\max_{i}\sup_{u\in\mathcal{N}(1/2)}\left|u^{\top}S_{W_{i}}\right|>t\right)\\
\leq N5^{m}p\exp\left(-\frac{(tN)^{\gamma}}{K_{1}^{\gamma}C_{4}}\right)+5^{m}p\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}N}{K_{1}^{2}C_{2}}\right).

Select

t=K_{1}\max\left\{C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{m\log(5p)}{N}},\frac{C_{4}}{N}\left(m\log 5NP\right)^{1/\gamma}\right\},

(F.11)

then we have

P\left(\|S_{W}\|_{\infty,A}>2c_{0}t\right)\leq 2\exp(-c^{\prime}m\log p),

for some constant
c^{\prime}>0
. Note that if
N=C_{1}(m\log p)^{2/\gamma-1}
, then the first term in (
F.11
) dominates because

C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{m\log(5p)}{N}}\geq\frac{C_{4}}{N}(m\log 5p)^{1/\gamma},\quad C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{m\log(5p)}{N}}\geq\frac{C_{4}}{N}(\log N)^{1/\gamma}.

Hence, if
C_{1}
is large enough, we have

C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{m\log(5p)}{N}}\geq\frac{C_{4}}{N}(m\log 5Np)^{1/\gamma}.

Then, we complete the proof.
∎

Appendix G
Proof of restricted eigenvalue condition

In this section, we want to verify that Assumption
6
holds under mild conditions. We begin with stating the main result.

Lemma G.1
.

Under the same conditions in Lemma
F.1
, and let

\mu=\sigma_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\Psi(t_{n})\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}\right]\right).

Define
C_{0},C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0
be universal constants and suppose that

N\geq\max\left\{\frac{C_{0}B^{2}m^{4}(\log p)^{3}}{\mu},C_{1}B^{5}\left(\frac{sm}{\mu}\right)^{5/2}\right\}.

Then for any
\nu\in\mathbb{R}^{pm}
, with probability at least
1-N\exp(-C_{2}ms\log p)
that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\left\{\nu\widehat{\Psi}(t_{n})\right\}^{2}\geq\alpha\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}-\tau\|\nu\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}^{2},

where

\alpha=\frac{\mu}{2},\quad\tau=C_{3}m\alpha\left\{\frac{(\log p)^{3}B^{2}}{\mu N}\right\}^{1/4}\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}\sigma_{\min}^{-2}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{i}}).

G.1
Proof of Lemma
G.1

Step 1: Uniform concentration over sparse vectors.

Define the set
\mathcal{K}(s)=\{\nu:\|\nu\|_{0}\leq s,\|\nu\|_{2}\leq 1\}
. Let
W(t_{n},\nu)=\sqrt{\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})}\nu^{\top}\Psi(t_{n})
for
\nu\in\mathcal{K}(2sm)
. By Lemma
I.3
, we have ∥W^2(t_n,
ν
)∥_
ψ
_1/2≤2^2∥W(t_n,
ν
)∥_
ψ
_1^2.
Apply Lemma
I.3
again, we have

\displaystyle\|W(t_{n},\nu)\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\widehat{w}^{1/2}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\|_{\psi_{2}}\|\nu^{\top}\Psi(t_{n})\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\nu\in\mathcal{K}(2sm)}\sup_{p\geq 1}p^{-1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}|\nu^{\top}\Psi(t_{n})|^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\nu\in\mathcal{K}(2sm)}\sup_{p\geq 1}p^{-1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\|\nu\|_{1}^{p}\|\Psi(t_{n})\|_{\infty}^{p}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq 2B\sqrt{2sm}.

Therefore, we can conclude that
W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)-\mathbb{E}[W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)]
is a Sub-Weibull(
\gamma_{2}
) variable with
\gamma_{2}=1/2
and

\left\|W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)-\mathbb{E}[W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)]\right\|_{\psi_{1/2}}\leq 2\|W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)\|_{\psi_{1/2}}\leq 64B^{2}sm.

Furthermore, following similar argument as
\emph{Step2}
in the proof of Lemma
F.1
,
W(t_{n},\nu)
is
\beta
-mixing with rate
\gamma_{1}=1
. Hence, we can compute
1/\gamma=1/\gamma_{1}+1/\gamma_{2}=1+2=3
. Apply Lemma
I.2
, we have

P\left(\left|N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)-\mathbb{E}[W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)]\right|\geq 2t\right)\leq N\exp\left\{\frac{-(2tN)^{\gamma}}{(64B^{2}sm)^{\gamma}C_{1}}\right\}+\exp\left\{\frac{-4t^{2}N}{(64B^{2}sm)^{2}C_{2}}\right\}.

Define
\mathcal{N}(1/2,\pi)
be a
1/2
-net on a support
\pi\in\Pi=\{\iota\subset\{i:i=1,\ldots p\}:|\iota|=2sm\}
. It follows that
|\Pi|=\binom{p}{2sm}
. Then taking union bound over all the possible
\nu
, we have

\displaystyle P\left(\sup_{\pi\in\Pi}\sup_{\nu\in\mathcal{N}(1/2,\pi)}\left|N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)-\mathbb{E}[W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)]\right|\geq t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{p}{2sm}5^{2sm}N\exp\left\{\frac{-(tN)^{\gamma}}{(32B^{2}sm)^{\gamma}C_{1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\binom{p}{2sm}5^{2sm}\exp\left\{\frac{-t^{2}N}{(32B^{2}sm)^{2}C_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{5ep}{2sm}\right)^{2sm}N\exp\left\{\frac{-(tN)^{\gamma}}{(32B^{2}sm)^{\gamma}C_{1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{5ep}{2sm}\right)^{2sm}\exp\left\{\frac{-t^{2}N}{(32B^{2}sm)^{2}C_{2}}\right\}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle P\left(\sup_{\nu\in K(2sm)}\left|N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)-\mathbb{E}[W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)]\right|\geq t\right)

\displaystyle\leq N\exp\left\{\frac{-(tN)^{\gamma}}{(32B^{2}s)^{\gamma}C_{1}}+C_{3}sm\log p\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\exp\left\{\frac{-t^{2}N}{(32B^{2}s)^{2}C_{2}}+C_{3}sm\log p\right\},

for some constant
C_{3}>0
.

Step 2: Uniform concentration over all vectors.
To find the uniform concentration on all vectors, we apply Lemma 12 in
Loh and Wainwright (2012)
, restated in Lemma
I.8
and obtain that

N^{-1}\left|\sum_{n=1}^{N}W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)-\mathbb{E}[W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)]\right|\geq 27t\left(\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{sm}\|\nu\|_{1}^{2}\right),

(G.1)

for any
\nu\in\mathbb{R}^{pm}
with probability at least

1-N\exp\left\{\frac{-(tN)^{\gamma}}{(32B^{2}sm)^{\gamma}C_{1}}+C_{3}sm\log p\right\}-\exp\left\{\frac{-t^{2}N}{(32B^{2}sm)^{2}C_{2}}+C_{3}sm\log p\right\}.

(G.2)

Recall that
\sigma_{\min}(N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}_{\Theta,\ell}(t_{n})\Psi(t_{n})\Psi(t_{n})^{\top}])\leq\mu
, we can obtain the following inequality from (
G.1
):

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)

\displaystyle\geq\mu\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}-27t\left(\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{sm}\|\nu\|_{1}^{2}\right).

Select
t=\mu/54
, the above display is equal to

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu}{2}\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\mu}{2sm}\|\nu\|_{1}^{2}.

Note that
\|\nu\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{m}\|\nu\|_{1,2}\leq\max_{i}(\sqrt{m}/\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{i}}))\|\nu\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}
, and hence we can further lower bound the above display as

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mu}{2}\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\mu}{2s}\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{i}})}\|\nu\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}^{2}.

Step 3: Select parameters.
We want

\frac{(tN)^{\gamma}}{(32B^{2}sm)^{\gamma}C_{1}}\leq\frac{t^{2}N}{(32B^{2}sm)^{2}C_{2}},

which implies that

N\geq\left(\frac{32B^{2}sm}{t}\right)^{(2-\gamma)/(1-\gamma)}\left(\frac{C_{2}}{C_{1}}\right)^{1/(1-\gamma)}=C_{4}B^{5}\left(\frac{sm}{\mu}\right)^{5/2},

where the last equality follows by plugging
\gamma=1/3
and
t=\mu/54
and
C_{4}=(24\sqrt{3})^{5}(C_{2}/C_{1})^{3/2}
. Furthermore, we want

2C_{3}sm\log p=\frac{(tN)^{\gamma}}{(32B^{2}sm)^{\gamma}C_{1}}

so that (
G.2
) is at least
1-2N\exp(-C_{3}sm\log p)
. This implies

\frac{1}{s}=m(24C_{1}C_{3})^{3/4}\left\{\frac{(\log p)^{3}B^{2}}{\mu N}\right\}^{1/4}.

Setting
s\geq 1
, we have

N\geq\frac{C_{5}B^{2}m^{4}(\log p)^{3}}{\mu},

where
C_{5}=(24C_{1}C_{3})^{3}
. Finally, let

N\geq\max\left\{\frac{C_{5}B^{2}m^{4}(\log p)^{3}}{\mu},C_{4}B^{5}\left(\frac{sm}{\mu}\right)^{5/2}\right\}.

Then,

N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}W^{2}(t_{n},\nu)\geq\alpha\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}-\tau\|\nu\|_{1,\widehat{K}_{\Psi}}^{2},

where
\alpha=\mu/2
,
\tau=m\alpha\{C_{5}(\log p)^{3}B^{2}/(\mu N)\}^{1/4}\max_{i}\sigma^{-2}_{\min}(\widehat{K}_{\Psi_{i}})
and with probability at least

1-2N\exp(-C_{3}ms\log p).

Appendix H
Proof of Theorem
4.5

Proof of Theorem
4.5
.

Before the start, we define a quantity

\displaystyle\delta_{e}=\max\left\{Nk\exp(-C_{8}ms\log p),k\exp(-C_{9}m\log p),(Npk+1)\exp(-C_{10}),k^{2}\exp(-C_{11})\right\}.

(H.1)

Step 1. Recovery of functions.
First, given fixed
i=1,\ldots,p
we choose
\delta=\delta_{e}/3p
for some constant
C_{0}
and apply Proposition
4.1

P\left(|\!|\!|\widehat{X}_{i}-X_{i}|\!|\!|^{2}>C_{0}\left(\frac{\log N+\log p}{N}\right)^{2\alpha/(1+2\alpha)}\right)\leq\frac{\delta_{e}}{p}.

Taking the union bound over all
i=1,\ldots,p
, we have

P\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}|\!|\!|\widehat{X}_{i}-X_{i}|\!|\!|>C_{0}\left(\frac{\log N+\log p}{N}\right)^{\alpha/(1+2\alpha)}\right)\leq\delta_{e}.

Since
N
satisfies

\frac{N}{\log N+\log p}\gtrsim\left(\frac{BDm}{c_{\Psi}}\right)^{(2\alpha+1)/\alpha}.

Then, we have

P\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,p}|\!|\!|\widehat{X}_{i}-X_{i}|\!|\!|>\frac{c_{\Psi}}{BDm}\right)\leq\delta_{e}.

Step 2. Induction on
\theta_{ij}^{\ell}
.

Now, we want to apply Lemma
H.1
. Suppose that we start with a point
\Theta^{0}
such that
\Theta^{(0)}\in B(r_{0},r_{q},r_{\sigma},\Theta^{\star})
. Given proposed choice of
N
and
r
, we want to apply Lemma
H.1
.
Define
\check{\Theta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\widetilde{\Theta}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\Theta}\mid\Theta^{(0)})
and

\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell}=(\check{\theta}_{i1}^{\ell\top},\ldots,\check{\theta}_{ip}^{\ell\top})
for
i=1,\ldots,p
.
We define similarly
\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}=({\theta}_{i1}^{\ell\star\top},\ldots,{\theta}_{ip}^{\ell\star\top})
,
\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell(0)}=({\theta}_{i1}^{\ell(0)\top},\ldots,{\theta}_{ip}^{\ell(0)\top})
for
i=1,\ldots,p
. Denote
\Theta^{(1)}=M_{n}(\Theta^{(0)})
and
apply Lemma
4.2
, we arrive at

\|\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}<\|{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell(0)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}.

Taking the maximum on both side,

\max_{i,\ell}\|\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}<\max_{i,\ell}\|{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell(0)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}.

Therefore, the choice of
\lambda
satisfies the condition required by Lemma
H.1
. Applying Lemma
H.1
, we have

\max_{i,\ell}\|{\theta}^{\ell(1)}_{i\cdot}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\leq{C_{3}c_{\Psi}^{-2}}\kappa\max_{i,\ell}\|{\theta}^{\ell(0)}_{i\cdot}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\\
+C_{4}\left\{\frac{m\sqrt{s\log p}}{\sqrt{N}}+ms\left(\frac{\log N+\log p}{N}\right)^{\alpha/(2\alpha+1)}+\sqrt{ms}\left(1-\zeta^{2}\pi_{\min}^{2}\right)^{r-1}\right\},

with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
. When
N,r
is large enough, we can guarantee that the right hand side is smaller than
p^{-1/2}r_{0}
. Therefore, we can apply Lemma
H.1
once more.

Similarly, denote
\Theta^{(2)}=M_{n}(\Theta^{(1)})
,
\Theta^{(1)}=M_{n}(\Theta^{(0)})
and
\check{\Theta}^{(1)}=M(\Theta^{(1)})
. First we want to check that
\lambda
satisfies the condition required by Lemma
H.1
. We denote

\lambda^{(1)}\geq\sqrt{m}\max\left\{C_{1}\left(1-\zeta\pi_{\min}^{2}\right)^{r-1},C_{1}\sqrt{ms}\left(\frac{\log N+\log p}{N}\right)^{\alpha/(2\alpha+1)},C_{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{ms}}\max_{i,\ell}\|\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell(1)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|\right\}.

We want to verify that
\lambda^{(1)}\leq\lambda
. This is equivalent as checking

\lambda\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\max_{i,\ell}\|\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell(1)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|.

(H.2)

Apply Proposition
4.2
, We can write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\|\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell(1)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{s}}\|{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell(1)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|.

Similar to (
H.8
), we can apply Lemma
4.3
and (
H.6
) and obtain the upper bound:

\displaystyle\leq\kappa 8c_{\Psi}^{-2}\left(10\lambda+\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\|\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|\right).

Apply Proposition
4.2
once more, we can upper bound the above term as

\displaystyle\leq\kappa 8c_{\Psi}^{-2}\left(10\lambda+\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{s}}\|{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell(0)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq(80c_{\Psi}^{-2}\kappa+\kappa^{2})\lambda.

Using the fact that
80c_{\Psi}^{-2}\kappa+\kappa^{2}<1
and taking the maximum on both side, we can conclude (
H.2
).

Iterate the EM-algorithm once more and apply Lemma
H.1
, we arrive at

\max_{i,\ell}\|{\theta}^{\ell(2)}_{i\cdot}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\leq\left(C_{3}c_{\Psi}^{-2}\kappa\right)^{2}\max_{i,\ell}\|{\theta}^{\ell(0)}_{i\cdot}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\\
+(1+C_{3}c_{\Psi}^{-2}\kappa)C_{4}\left\{\frac{m\sqrt{s\log p}}{\sqrt{N}}+ms\left(\frac{\log N+\log p}{N}\right)^{\alpha/(2\alpha+1)}+\sqrt{ms}\left(1-\zeta^{2}\pi_{\min}^{2}\right)^{r-1}\right\},

with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
.

Hence, if we update the EM-algorithm for
L
times, we will have

\max_{i,\ell}\|{\theta}^{\ell(L)}_{i\cdot}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\leq\left(C_{3}c_{\Psi}^{-2}\kappa\right)^{L}\max_{i,\ell}\|{\theta}^{\ell(0)}_{i\cdot}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\\
+\frac{C_{4}}{1-C_{3}c_{\Psi}^{-2}\kappa}\left\{\frac{m\sqrt{s\log p}}{\sqrt{N}}+ms\left(\frac{\log N+\log p}{N}\right)^{\alpha/(2\alpha+1)}+\sqrt{ms}\left(1-\zeta^{2}\pi_{\min}^{2}\right)^{r-1}\right\},

with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
.

Step 3. Induction on
\sigma
.
Apply Lemma
H.2
and Proposition
4.2
, we arrive at

\left|{\sigma^{(1)}}^{2}-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|\leq\frac{C_{5}c_{\Psi}^{-2}msr_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{N}}+C_{6}k\zeta^{-8}\pi_{\min}^{-2}\left\{1-(\zeta\pi_{\min})^{2}\right\}^{r-1}+\kappa\left|{\sigma^{(0)}}^{2}-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|,

with probability at least
1-\delta_{e}
. Since, from
Step 2
, we know that
\sum_{i}\|\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell(1)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}^{2}\leq r_{0}^{2}
with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
. Therefore we can apply Lemma
H.2
and Proposition
4.2
again. Repeat the steps for
L-2
times, we will have

\left|{\sigma^{(L)}}^{2}-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|\leq\kappa^{L}\left|{\sigma^{(0)}}^{2}-\sigma^{\star 2}\right|+\frac{1}{1-\kappa}\left[\frac{C_{5}c_{\Psi}^{-2}msr_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{N}}+C_{6}k\zeta^{-8}\pi_{\min}^{-2}\left\{1-(\zeta\pi_{\min})^{2}\right\}^{r-1}\right],

with probability at least
1-3\delta_{e}
.

Step 4. Induction on
Q
.
Apply Lemma
E.5
to Lemma
E.2
, we see that

\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}\left|{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{(1)}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|\leq\frac{C_{7}k(k-1)}{\sqrt{N}}+\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}\left|\check{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|.

(H.3)

with probability at least
1-k^{2}\exp(-C_{5})\leq 1-\delta_{e}
.
Note that, we can apply Proposition
4.2
to the right hand side of (
H.3
), and obtain

\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}\left|{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{(1)}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|\leq\frac{C_{7}k(k-1)}{\sqrt{N}}+\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}\kappa\left|{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{(0)}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|,

(H.4)

with probability at least
1-\delta_{e}
.
Noting that
N
is selected large enough such that the right hand side of (
H.4
) is bounded by
r_{q}
. Hence repeat the above steps to analyze the distance of
\Theta^{(2)}=M_{n}(\Theta^{(1)})
to
\Theta^{\star}
. Then, we repeat the above steps for
L-2
more times, we arrive at

\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}\left|{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{(L)}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|\leq\frac{1}{1-\kappa}\frac{C_{7}k(k-1)}{\sqrt{N}}+\sum_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}\kappa^{L}\left|{q}_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{(0)}-q_{\ell\ell^{\prime}}^{\star}\right|,

with probability at least
1-\delta_{e}
.

∎

H.1
Proof of Corollary
4.6

Proof of Corollary
4.6
.

Since

L\geq\frac{\log\epsilon_{t}-\log 4r_{0}}{\log(C_{3}c_{\Psi}^{-2}\kappa)},

and under the assumptions of Corollary
4.6
, it follows that

\max_{i,\ell}\|\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell(L)}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{t},

with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
. This implies that

\|\theta_{ij}^{\ell(L)}-\theta_{ij}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\geq\|\theta_{ij}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{t},

with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
. Note that if
\|\theta_{ij}^{\ell\star}\|\neq 0
, namely
(j,i)\in E^{\ell}
, then
\|\theta_{ij}^{\ell\star}\|\geq(3/2)\epsilon_{t}
by construction of
\epsilon_{t}
. Therefore, for every
(j,i)\in E^{\ell}
, then

\|\theta_{ij}^{\ell(L)}\|_{2}\geq\epsilon_{t},

with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
. Hence,
(j,i)\in\widehat{E}^{\ell}
with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
. In contrast, for every
(j,i)\not\in E^{\ell}

\|\theta_{ij}^{\ell(L)}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{t},

with probability at least
1-2\delta_{e}
.
∎

H.2
Proof of Lemma
H.1

Lemma H.1
.

Assume Assumption
2
,
3
,
5
,
8
–
10
hold and let
C_{1},\ldots,C_{6}
to be some universal constants.
Suppose that
Z(0)
is sampled from the stationary distribution. Define
\check{\Theta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\widetilde{\Theta}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\Theta}\mid\Theta)
and
\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell}=(\check{\theta}_{i1}^{\ell\top},\ldots,\check{\theta}_{ip}^{\ell\top})
for

. Let

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\max_{i,j}\left|g_{j}(X_{i}(t))\right|\leq B,\quad\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\max_{i,j}\left|\dot{g}_{j}(X_{i}(t))\right|\leq D,

.
Suppose that
N\gtrsim\left\{m^{4}(\log p)^{3}\vee m^{5/2}s^{5/2}\right\}

\max_{i}|\!|\!|\widehat{X}_{i}-X_{i}|\!|\!|=\delta_{1}\leq c_{\Psi}(4BDm)^{-1}

\lambda\geq C_{1}\sqrt{m}\max\left\{\left(1-\zeta\pi_{\min}^{2}\right)^{r-1},\sqrt{ms}\delta_{1},\sqrt{\frac{m\log p}{N}}\right\}\vee\frac{C_{2}}{\sqrt{s}}\max_{i,\ell}\|\check{\theta}_{i\cdot}^{\ell}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|

\max_{i,\ell}\|\widehat{\theta}^{\ell}_{i\cdot}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}\leq{C_{3}c_{\Psi}^{-2}}\kappa\max_{i,\ell}\|{\theta}^{\ell}_{i\cdot}-\theta_{i\cdot}^{\ell\star}\|_{2}+C_{4}\left\{\frac{m\sqrt{s\log p}}{\sqrt{N}}+ms\delta_{1}+\sqrt{ms}\left(1-\zeta^{2}\pi_{\min}^{2}\right)^{r-1}\right\},

with probability at least
